Tensor enriched categorical generalization of the Eilenberg-Watts theorem
Jaehyeok Lee

Abstract
Let b b , b ′ b^{\prime} be commutative monoids in a Bénabou cosmos. Motivated by six-functor formalisms in algebraic geometry, we prove that the category of commutative monoids over b ⊗ b ′ b\otimes b^{\prime} is equivalent to the category of cocontinuous lax monoidal enriched functors between the monoidal enriched categories of right modules over b b , b ′ b^{\prime} .

1
Introduction

A six-functor formalism is an assignment

\mathpzc{X}\mapsto\mathcal{D}(\mathpzc{X})

which sends each geometric object
\mathpzc{X}

to a category
\mathcal{D}(\mathpzc{X})
,
such that for each morphism

f:\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}\to\mathpzc{X}

of geometric objects
we have six functors

f^{*}
,
f_{*}
,

\otimes
,
\underline{\textit{Hom}}
,

f_{!}
,
f^{!}

between

\mathcal{D}(\mathpzc{X})
,
\mathcal{D}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})

satisfying certain properties.
The category
\mathcal{D}(\mathpzc{X})

is often a triangulated category or a stable
\infty
-category.
Such formalism was first formulated during the development of étale cohomology groups of schemes,
as it contains the structural properties of cohomology groups.
Six-functor formalisms are realized, or expected to exist
in various contexts throughout geometry and topology.
We refer to
Scholze 2022

for a detailed explanation, examples and recent developments
of six-functor formalisms.

Consider the case of affine schemes
\mathpzc{X}

and the categories
\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})
of their quasi-coherent sheaves.
For each morphism

f:\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}\to\mathpzc{X}

of affine schemes, we have a pair of adjunctions

f^{*}\dashv f_{*}

,

f_{!}\dashv f^{!}

between
\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})
,
\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})

and a natural isomorphism

f_{!}\cong f_{*}
.
Furthermore,

f^{*}:\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})\to\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})

is a symmetric monoidal functor
and the lax symmetric monoidal adjunction

f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
satisfies the projection formula.
For each pair of morphisms

\mathpzc{X}\leftarrow\tilde{\mathpzc{X}}\to\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}

of affine schemes,
which is a correspondence from
\mathpzc{X}
to
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}
,
we associate a cocontinuous lax symmetric monoidal functor

\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})\to\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})

as follows.

\vbox{\hbox{
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.66666pt\raise 4.46324pt\raise-4.91992pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise-17.2604pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.39346pt\raise-3.91965pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.30557pt\raise-16.43698pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 51.7917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{ }}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-22.87003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathpzc{X}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 22.86113pt\raise-22.87003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\text{ }}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.30557pt\raise-22.87003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}\qquad\mapsto\qquad\vbox{\hbox{
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 23.16106pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.16106pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.29639pt\raise 6.57835pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.16106pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.16106pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}

(1.1)

The six functors
associated to each morphism of affine schemes
are additive functors
(i.e., enriched in the closed symmetric monoidal category
\mathlcal{Ab}
of abelian groups),
and their symmetric monoidal structures are compatible with additive structures.
It turns out that if we impose this compatibility condition,
(
1.1
)
becomes an equivalence of categories.
This is an immediate consequence of the result of this paper.

The main result of this paper states that the assignment
(
1.1
)
defines an equivalence of categories,
not only for affine schemes,
but for commutative monoids in an abstract symmetric monoidal category.
A
Bénabou cosmos

(\mathcal{V},\otimes,c)

is a closed symmetric monoidal category
whose underlying category is complete and cocomplete.
Such monoidal category
\mathcal{V}
has enough properties
so that a large amount of the theory of ordinary categories extends
to the theory of categories enriched in
\mathcal{V}
:
see
(
Street 2006
, p. 134)
.
We present the main result.

Theorem
5.3
.

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids
in a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}

and let

\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

be the symmetric monoidal
\mathcal{V}
-enriched categories of right modules over
b
,
b^{\prime}
.
We have a fully faithful left adjoint functor

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{b\xrightarrow{f}\tilde{b}\xleftarrow{f^{\prime}}b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

from the category of commutative monoids
in
\mathcal{V}
over
b\otimes b^{\prime}

to the category of lax symmetric monoidal
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
The essential image of this functor
is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

of cocontinuous lax symmetric monoidal
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
and we have an adjoint equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\text{ }\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

The adjoint equivalence of categories
in Theorem
5.3

for the case
\mathcal{V}=\mathlcal{Ab}
tells us that
affine schemes and their morphisms can be understood
in terms of their associated six functors.
As this point of view towards affine schemes is immediately generalized to
commutative monoids in an arbitrary Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}
,
the author expects that certain topics in algebraic geometry
can be developed over any
\mathcal{V}
.

Brandenburg and Chirvasitu

(
Brandenburg and Chirvasitu 2014
, Theorem 3.4.3)

showed that given a quasi-compact quasi-separated scheme

\mathpzc{X}
and an arbitrary scheme
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}
,
the assignment
f\mapsto f^{*}

defines an equivalence
from the discrete category
of morphisms
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}\to\mathpzc{X}

of schemes
to the category of cocontinuous strong symmetric monoidal functors

\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})\to\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})
.
The following, which is a consequence of Theorem
5.3
,
generalizes the particular case of

(
Brandenburg and Chirvasitu 2014
, Theorem 3.4.3)

when schemes
\mathpzc{X}
,
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}
are affine.

Theorem
5.4
.

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids
in a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}

and let

\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

be the symmetric monoidal
\mathcal{V}
-enriched categories of right modules over
b
,
b^{\prime}
.
We have an equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\Hom_{\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})}(b,b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{strong}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{f:b\to b^{\prime}\text{ }\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\text{ }f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

from the discrete category of morphisms

b\to b^{\prime}
of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}

to the category of cocontinuous strong symmetric monoidal
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.

The well-known theorem of
Eilenberg
Eilenberg 1960
and Watts
Watts 1960

states that every cocontinuous functor

\textit{Mod}_{R}\to\textit{Mod}_{R^{\prime}}

between the categories of right modules over rings
R
,
R^{\prime}

is naturally isomorphic to

-\otimes_{R}N:\textit{Mod}_{R}\to\textit{Mod}_{R^{\prime}}

for some
(R,R^{\prime})
-bimodule
N
.
We introduce the following generalization of the Eilenberg-Watts theorem,
which serves as a midpoint towards Theorem
5.3
.

Theorem
3.3
.

Let
b
be a monoid in a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}

and let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete
\mathcal{V}
-enriched category.
We have a fully faithful left adjoint functor

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{-\circledast X:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}}

from the category of left
b
-module objects in
\mathcal{C}

to the category of
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
.
The essential image of this functor
is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})

of cocontinuous
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
,
and we have an adjoint equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Although there are some minor improvements,
the statement and the proof of Theorem
3.3

are mostly due to the work of Hovey in

(
Hovey 2009
, §1-§2)
.
We are going to follow Hovey’s approach and
present a detailed proof of Theorem
3.3
,
which was a necessary step for the author to obtain Theorem
5.3
.

We list some generalizations of the Eilenberg-Watts theorem
presented in the literature,
and explain how they are related to
the results in this paper.

•

In the theory of categories enriched in a Bénabou cosmos,
the Eilenberg-Watts theorem holds in great generality
where rings, modules and bimodules can be replaced by
small enriched categories, enriched presheaves and enriched profunctors.

1
1

1

It is stated as Theorem 2.5 in the nLab page,
https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/Eilenberg-Watts+theorem.

A variant of this is the equivalence in Theorem
3.3
.

•

In
(
Hovey 2009
, Proposition 1.1)

Hovey shows that for each
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
we have a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
\mathcal{F}(b)

and a natural isomorphism

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.
In
(
Hovey 2009
, Theorem 2.3)

he also gives a characterization of
\mathcal{F}

whose associated
\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}
is a natural isomorphism.
These results,
together with an additional property that

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}
is enriched in
\mathcal{V}
,
are presented with details in this paper as
Proposition
3.2
.

•

In
Nyman 2010

Nyman studies obstructions to
an
R
-linear cocontinuous functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X})\to\textit{QCoh}(\mathpzc{X}^{\prime})

between the categories of quasi-coherent sheaves
on
R
-schemes
\mathpzc{X}
,
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}

being isomorphic to tensoring with a bimodule.
Main difference with our results
is that we are interested in characterizing
which lax symmetric monoidal
functor
\mathcal{F}

is isomorphic to
f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}

for some correspondence from

\mathpzc{X}
to
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}
.
Another difference is that we consider
\mathpzc{X}
,
\mathpzc{X}^{\prime}

to be affine schemes,
but defined internal to a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}
.

•

In
(
Femic 2016
, Theorem 3.4)

Femić proves the Eilenberg-Watts theorem for

2
-categories of the representation categories

\mathscr{C}\textit{-Mod}

of finite tensor categories
\mathscr{C}
.
The author does not see any precise connection
between
(
Femic 2016
, Theorem 3.4)

and the results presented in this paper.

Summary.

We summarize the content as follows.
The author would like to mention that
the paper is partially expository
and contains details of proofs.

•

In §​
2

we fix a Bénabou cosmos
(\mathcal{V},\otimes,c)

and explain notations, terminologies
as well as elementary categorical tools
used in the paper.
Every enriched structure is considered as enriched in
\mathcal{V}
.

•

In §​
3

we present a detailed proof of Theorem
3.3
,
following the approach introduced in
(
Hovey 2009
, §1-§2)
.
Let
b
be a monoid in
\mathcal{V}

and let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete enriched category.
For each enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
,
the image
\mathcal{F}(b)

of the right
b
-module
b
has a canonical left
b
-action.
The main step is to show that
we have an enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

(1.2)

between enriched functors
\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
,
which is an enriched natural isomorphism
if and only if

\mathcal{F}
is cocontinuous as an enriched functor.
After proving Theorem
3.3
,
we explain the result when

\mathcal{C}=\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

for another monoid
b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{V}
.

•

In §​
4

we establish the six functors
associated to each morphism of commutative monoids

f:b\to b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{V}
.
We describe the closed symmetric monoidal
enriched category

(\textit{Mod}_{b},\circledast,b)
,
the pair of enriched adjunctions

f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
,
f_{!}\dashv f^{!}
,
the enriched natural isomorphism

f_{!}\cong f_{*}

and the lax symmetric monoidal enriched adjunction

f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
satisfying the projection formula.

•

In §​
5

we prove Theorem
5.3

as well as Theorem
5.4
.
Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
Each lax symmetric monoidal enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

determines a pair of morphisms

f:b\to\mathcal{F}(b)
,

f^{\prime}:b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
The main step is to show that
the enriched natural transformation (
1.2
)
becomes a monoidal enriched natural transformation

f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

•

In §​
6

we provide another method of
obtaining the equivalence of categories
in Theorem
5.3
.
The main observation is that
cocontinuous lax symmetric monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

are identified with commutative monoids in the category
of cocontinuous enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

with respect to Day convolution.

•

In the appendix §​
A
,
we provide details which were omitted in proofs.

The author would like to end this section
with an explanation of the title of the paper.
By ‘Tensor enriched categorical’
it means two categorical structures.
One is symmetric monoidal enriched categorical structures.
The other is tensored enriched categorical structures,
such as tensorial strengths associated to
enriched functors between tensored enriched categories.

2
Preliminaries

A
symmetric monoidal category

is a tuple
(\mathcal{C},\circledast,\textit{1};a,\imath,\jmath,s)

of a category
\mathcal{C}
,
a functor
\circledast:\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
,
a distinguished object
1
in
\mathcal{C}
called the
unit object
,
and isomorphisms

Z\circledast(X\circledast Y)\xrightarrow[\cong]{a_{Z,X,Y}}(Z\circledast X)\circledast Y,\qquad\begin{aligned} &\textit{1}\circledast X\xrightarrow[\cong]{\imath_{X}}X,\\
&X\circledast\textit{1}\xrightarrow[\cong]{\jmath_{X}}X,\end{aligned}\qquad X\circledast Y\xrightarrow[\cong]{s_{X,Y}}Y\circledast X,\qquad Z,X,Y\in\mathcal{C}

in
\mathcal{C}
called the

coherence isomorphisms

which are natural in variables
X
,
Y
,
Z\in\mathcal{C}

and satisfy the pentagon, unity, symmetry and hexagon axioms.
We denote a symmetric monoidal category

(\mathcal{C},\circledast,\textit{1};a,\imath,\jmath,s)

as
\mathcal{C}
when the rest of the structures are clear in the given context.
We say a symmetric monoidal category

\mathcal{C}
is
closed

if for each object
X
in
\mathcal{C}
,
the functor
-\circledast X:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

admits a right adjoint
[X,-]:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a symmetric monoidal category.
A diagram in
\mathcal{C}
is called
formal

when it only consists of the coherence isomorphisms

a
,
\imath
,
\jmath
,
s
,
their inverses
and monoidal products of these isomorphisms with identity morphisms.
The coherence theorem, proved by S. Mac Lane in
Mac Lane 1963
,
states that every formal diagram in
\mathcal{C}
commutes.
We will use this fact freely.

Let
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
be symmetric monoidal categories.
A
lax (symmetric) monoidal functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{0},\mathcal{F}^{2}):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

is a tuple of a functor

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
,
a morphism

\mathcal{F}^{0}:\textit{1}\to\mathcal{F}(\textit{1})

in
\mathcal{D}

and a collection of morphisms

\mathcal{F}^{2}_{X,Y}:\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(X\circledast Y),\qquad X,Y\in\mathcal{C}

in
\mathcal{D}

which is natural in variables
X
,
Y
,
such that the morphisms
\mathcal{F}^{0}
,
\mathcal{F}^{2}

are compatible with coherence isomorphisms

a
,
\imath
,
\jmath
,
s
of
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
We denote a lax monoidal functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{0},\mathcal{F}^{2})

as
\mathcal{F}
,
and the morphism
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{X,Y}
as
\mathcal{F}^{2}

when the meaning is clear in context.
A
colax (symmetric) monoidal functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

is similarly defined, but the morphisms

\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0}:\mathcal{F}(\textit{1})\to\textit{1}
,

\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{X,Y}:\mathcal{F}(X\circledast Y)\to\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y)

in
\mathcal{D}
are in the opposite direction.
Again, we denote a colax monoidal functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2})

as
\mathcal{F}
,
and the morphism
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{X,Y}
as
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}

when the meaning is clear in context.
A
strong (symmetric) monoidal functor

from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}

is a lax monoidal functor

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

whose morphisms

\mathcal{F}^{0}
,
\mathcal{F}^{2}
in
\mathcal{D}

are isomorphisms.

Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be lax monoidal functors between symmetric monoidal categories
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
A
monoidal natural transformation

from
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{G}

is a natural transformation
\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}

which is compatible with
\mathcal{F}^{0}
,
\mathcal{G}^{0}
and
\mathcal{F}^{2}
,
\mathcal{G}^{2}
.
For detailed explanations of symmetric monoidal categories,
lax/colax/strong monoidal functors and monoidal natural transformations,
see
(
Johnson and Yau 2021
, §1.2)
.

We refer

Kelly 2005
,
(
Borceux 1994
, §6)

for the basics of enriched category theory.
A
Bénabou cosmos

is a closed symmetric monoidal category

(\mathcal{V},\otimes,c;a,\imath,\jmath,s)

whose underlying category

\mathcal{V}
is complete and cocomplete.
Some examples of Bénabou cosmoi are
the closed symmetric monoidal categories

\mathlcal{Set},\text{ }\mathlcal{Cat},\text{ }\mathlcal{sSet},\text{ }\mathlcal{Mod}_{R},\text{ }\mathlcal{Ch}_{R},\text{ }\mathlcal{CGWH},\text{ }\mathlcal{S\!p}^{\Sigma},\text{ }\mathlcal{Ba\!n}

of
(small) sets,
simplicial sets,
categories
modules over a commutative ring
R
,
(co)chain complexes over a commutative ring
R
,
compactly generated weakly Hausdorff spaces,
symmetric spectra,
of Banach spaces with linear contractions.
Every Grothendieck topos is also a Bénabou cosmos.

Throughout this paper,
we fix a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}
.
Objects in the fixed Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}

are denoted with small letters
w
,
x
,
y
,
z\in\mathcal{V}
.
For instance, the unit object is denoted as
c\in\mathcal{V}
.
Every enriched structure in this paper
is considered as enriched in
\mathcal{V}
.
We use the same notations for
enriched categorical structures and their underlying (ordinary) category structures.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an enriched category
and let
X
,
Y
,
Z
be objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
By a morphism in
\mathcal{C}

we mean a morphism
X\to Y
in the underlying category of
\mathcal{C}
.
We denote the Hom object from
X
to
Y
as

\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\in\mathcal{V}
,
and the identity, composition morphisms in
\mathcal{V}
as

\mdmathbb{I}_{X}:X\to X
,
\mu_{X,Y,Z}:\mathcal{C}(Y,Z)\otimes\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\to\mathcal{C}(X,Z)
.
Let
\mathcal{D}
be another enriched category
and let
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be an enriched functor.
We denote the induced morphism between Hom objects as

\mathcal{F}_{X,Y}:\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{F}(X),\mathcal{F}(Y)).

Let
\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be another enriched functor and let

\xi:\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}

be an enriched natural transformation.
The component of
\xi
at each object
X
in
\mathcal{C}

is denoted as
\xi_{X}:\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{G}(X)
.

A
(symmetric) monoidal enriched category

is a tuple
(\mathcal{C},\circledast,\textit{1};a,\imath,\jmath,s)

of an enriched category
\mathcal{C}
,
an enriched functor
\circledast:\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
,
a distinguished object
1
in
\mathcal{C}

and enriched natural isomorphisms

a
,
\imath
,
\jmath
,
s

satisfying the pentagon, unity, symmetry and hexagon axioms.
Again, we denote a monoidal enriched category

(\mathcal{C},\circledast,\textit{1};a,\imath,\jmath,s)

as
\mathcal{C}
when there is no confusion in meaning about the rest of the structures.
A monoidal enriched category
\mathcal{C}
is called
closed

if for each object
X
in
\mathcal{C}

the enriched functor
-\circledast X:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}

has a left enriched adjoint
[X,-]:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
.

We can enhance
\mathcal{V}
to a closed monoidal enriched category
which is complete and cocomplete as an enriched category.
Let
x
,
y
be objects in
\mathcal{V}
.
The Hom object is given by
\mathcal{V}(x,y)=[x,y]
,
and the enriched functor
-\otimes x:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}

is left adjoint to the Hom enriched functor

\mathcal{V}(x,-):\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{V}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}

be monoidal enriched categories.
A
lax (symmetric) monoidal enriched functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{0},\mathcal{F}^{2}):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

is a tuple of an enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
,
a morphism

\mathcal{F}^{0}:\textit{1}\to\mathcal{F}(\textit{1})

in
\mathcal{D}

and a collection of morphisms

\mathcal{F}^{2}_{X,Y}:\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y)\to\mathcal{F}(X\circledast Y)

in
\mathcal{D}

which is enriched natural in variables
X
,
Y\in\mathcal{C}
,
such that
\mathcal{F}^{0}
,
\mathcal{F}^{2}

are compatible with coherence isomorphisms of
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
We also denote a lax monoidal enriched functor

(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{0},\mathcal{F}^{2})

as
\mathcal{F}
,
and the morphism
\mathcal{F}^{2}_{X,Y}
as
\mathcal{F}^{2}
.
We omit the explanation of
colax/strong (symmetric) monoidal enriched functors
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be lax monoidal enriched functors between
monoidal enriched categories
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
A
monoidal enriched natural transformation

from
\mathcal{F}
to
\mathcal{G}

is an enriched natural transformation
\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}

which is compatible with
\mathcal{F}^{0}
,
\mathcal{G}^{0}
and
\mathcal{F}^{2}
,
\mathcal{G}^{2}
.

A
lax (symmetric) monoidal enriched adjunction

is an adjunction internal to the
2
-category of
monoidal enriched categories,
lax monoidal enriched functors
and monoidal enriched natural transformations.
A pair of lax monoidal enriched functors

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
,

\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}

between monoidal enriched categories
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}

forms a lax monoidal enriched adjunction

\mathcal{F}\dashv\mathcal{G}

if there exists unit, counit monoidal enriched natural transformations

\eta:\id_{\mathcal{C}}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}\mathcal{F}
,

\varepsilon:\mathcal{F}\mathcal{G}\Rightarrow\id_{\mathcal{D}}

which satisfy the triangle identities.
We say a lax monoidal enriched adjunction

\mathcal{F}\dashv\mathcal{G}

satisfies the projection formula
if
the following composition is an isomorphism
for all
X\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathcal{D}
.

\textstyle{X\circledast\mathcal{G}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{X}\circledast I_{\mathcal{G}(A)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{G}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{G}^{2}_{\mathcal{F}(X),A}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast A)}

(2.1)

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
be monoidal enriched categories
and let

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{G}}

be an enriched adjunction
with unit, counit enriched natural transformations

\eta
,
\varepsilon
.
Suppose the right enriched adjoint

\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}

has a structure of a lax monoidal enriched functor.

1.

The left enriched adjoint

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

becomes a colax monoidal enriched functor,
whose morphisms
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0}
,
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}

are given as follows. Let
X
,
Y\in\mathcal{C}
.

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(\textit{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{G}^{0})}
\scriptstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{G}(\textit{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\textit{1}}}
1

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(X\circledast Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\eta_{X}\circledast\eta_{Y})}
\scriptstyle{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{X,Y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{G}\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{G}\mathcal{F}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{G}^{2}_{\mathcal{F}(X),\mathcal{F}(Y)})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(X)\circledast\mathcal{F}(Y)}

2.

If the morphisms

\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0}
,
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2}

are isomorphisms,
then
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

is a strong monoidal enriched functor with

\mathcal{F}^{0}=(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{0})^{-1}
,

\mathcal{F}^{2}=(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{2})^{-1}

and
\mathcal{F}\dashv\mathcal{G}
is a lax monoidal enriched adjunction.

Remark 2.2
.

Lemma
2.1

follows directly from Kelly’s work

Kelly 1974

about doctrinal adjunctions,
by applying it to a
2
-monad on the
2
-category of enriched categories
whose algebras are monoidal enriched categories.

For the rest of this section,
we recall the properties of tensorial strengths
associated to enriched functors
between tensored enriched categories.
See
(
Segrt Ratkovic 2013
, §3)
for a detailed explanation.
An enriched category
\mathcal{C}
is called
tensored

if for each object
X
in
\mathcal{C}

the Hom enriched functor

\mathcal{C}(X,-):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{V}

admits a left enriched adjoint

-\otimes X:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{C}
.
We denote the components of
the adjunction unit, counit enriched natural transformations
at
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
Y\in\mathcal{C}
as

\vbox{\hbox{
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.72672pt\raise 10.11389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.975pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{-\otimes X}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.95587pt\raise-10.80554pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise-4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}\qquad\textit{Cv}_{z,X}:z\to\mathcal{C}(X,z\otimes X),\qquad\textit{Ev}_{X,Y}:\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\otimes X\to Y.

(2.2)

We have an isomorphism

\imath_{X}:c\otimes X\xrightarrow{\cong}X
in
\mathcal{C}

which corresponds to the morphism

\mdmathbb{I}_{X}:c\to\mathcal{C}(X,X)
in
\mathcal{V}
.

Let

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be an enriched functor
between tensored enriched categories

\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
The
tensorial strength
associated to
\mathcal{F}

is the enriched natural transformation

t^{\mathcal{F}}:-\otimes\mathcal{F}(-)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\otimes-)

between enriched functors from
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
,
whose component at

z\in\mathcal{V}
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
is the unique morphism

t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,X}:z\otimes\mathcal{F}(X)\to\mathcal{F}(z\otimes X)

in
\mathcal{D}
which corresponds to the
morphism

\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{Cv}_{z,X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,z\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{X,z\otimes X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{F}(X),\mathcal{F}(z\otimes X))}

in
\mathcal{V}
.
We say the given enriched functor

\mathcal{F}

preserves tensored objects

if the associated tensorial strength

t^{\mathcal{F}}
is an enriched natural isomorphism.

Let
X
be an object in a tensored enriched category
\mathcal{C}
.
We denote the tensorial strength associated to the cocontinuous enriched functor

-\otimes X:\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{C}
as

a_{w,z,X}=t^{-\otimes X}_{w,z}:w\otimes(z\otimes X)\xrightarrow{\cong}(w\otimes z)\otimes X,\qquad w,z\in\mathcal{V}

which is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}
.
We often omit
a_{w,z,X}

and simply write as
w\otimes z\otimes X
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
be tensored enriched categories.
For each enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
,
the associated tensorial strength
t^{\mathcal{F}}

satisfies the following relations for all
w
,
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
.

\textstyle{c\otimes\mathcal{F}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{c,X}}
\scriptstyle{\imath_{\mathcal{F}(X)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(c\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\imath_{X})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(X)}

\textstyle{w\otimes(z\otimes\mathcal{F}(X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{w,z,\mathcal{F}(X)}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,X}}
\textstyle{w\otimes\mathcal{F}(z\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{w,z\otimes X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(w\otimes(z\otimes X))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(a_{w,z,X})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\otimes\mathcal{F}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{w\otimes z,X}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}((w\otimes z)\otimes X)}

(2.3)

Conversely, suppose we have a functor

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
between the underlying categories
and a natural transformation

t^{\mathcal{F}}:-\otimes\mathcal{F}(-)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\otimes-)

between functors from
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}

that satisfies the relations (
2.3
).
Then there is a unique enrichment of
\mathcal{F}

whose associated tensorial strength is equal to
the given natural transformation
t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,X}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}

be enriched functors between tensored enriched categories
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
.
For each enriched natural transformation

\xi:\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}
,
the following relation holds for
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
.

\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\xi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{z\otimes X}}
\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{G}}_{z,X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}(z\otimes X)}

(2.4)

Conversely, a natural transformation

\xi:\mathcal{F}\Rightarrow\mathcal{G}

between the underlying functors
becomes an enriched natural transformation
if and only if it satisfies the relation (
2.4
) for all

z\in\mathcal{V}
,
X\in\mathcal{C}
.

3
Eilenberg-Watts theorem in the enriched setting

A
monoid
in
\mathcal{V}

is a tuple
(b,u_{b},m_{b})

of an object
b
in
\mathcal{V}

and unit, product morphisms
u_{b}:c\to b
,
m_{b}:b\otimes b\to b

in
\mathcal{V}
satisfying the associativity, unital relations.
We will abbreviate the unit, product morphisms
and denote a monoid
(b,u_{b},m_{b})
in
\mathcal{V}
as
b
.
Throughout this section,
we fix a monoid
b
in
\mathcal{V}

and denote

\textit{Mod}_{b}

as the complete, cocomplete enriched category
of right
b
-modules.
An object in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

is a pair
(x,{}_{x})

of an object
x
in
\mathcal{V}
,
and a morphism

{}_{x}:x\otimes b\to x

in
\mathcal{V}
that satisfies the right
b
-action relations.
We will omit the right
b
-action and simply denote
a right
b
-module
(x,{}_{x})
as
x
.
Let
x
,
y
be right
b
-modules.
The Hom object is given by the equalizer

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}(x,y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{eq}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}(x,y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{V}({}_{x},\mdmathbb{I}_{y})}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{V}(\mdmathbb{I}_{x},{}_{y})\circ(-\otimes b)_{x,y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}(x\otimes b,y)}

and for
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
the tensored object with
x
is

z\otimes x
whose right
b
-action is

\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes{}_{x}
.
We can see
b
itself as a right
b
-module,
whose right
b
-action is the product morphism
m_{b}
.
The right
b
-action morphism
{}_{x}:x\otimes b\to x
of
x

becomes a morphism in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

For the rest of this section,
let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete enriched category.
A
left
b
-module object in
\mathcal{C}

is a pair
(X,\rho_{X})

of an object
X
in
\mathcal{C}
,
and a morphism
\rho_{X}:b\otimes X\to X
in
\mathcal{C}

that satisfies the left
b
-action relations.
We omit the left
b
-action morphism and denote
a left
b
-module object
(X,\rho_{X})
in
\mathcal{C}

as
X
.
The morphism in
\mathcal{V}

corresponding to
\rho_{X}:b\otimes X\to X

is denoted as

\bar{\rho}_{X}:b\to\mathcal{C}(X,X)
.
A morphism
X\to Y

of left
b
-module objects in
\mathcal{C}

is a morphism in
\mathcal{C}

which is compatible with left
b
-actions
\rho_{X}
,
\rho_{Y}
.
We denote

\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}

as the category of left
b
-module objects in
\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
X
be an object in
\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}
.
We have an enriched adjunction

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\circledast X}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,-)}

(3.1)

which is a version of a Hom-tensor adjunction.

1.

The left enriched adjoint

-\circledast X:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

sends each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

to the following coequalizer in
\mathcal{C}
.

\textstyle{z\otimes b\otimes X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\rho_{X}}
\textstyle{z\otimes X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,X}}
\textstyle{z\circledast X}

2.

The right enriched adjoint

\mathcal{C}(X,-):\mathcal{C}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

sends each
Y\in\mathcal{C}
to the object

\mathcal{C}(X,Y)
in
\mathcal{V}
equipped with
the right
b
-action morphism

{}_{\mathcal{C}(X,Y)}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.24994pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.24994pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\otimes b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.44002pt\raise 7.13333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.54445pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{\mathcal{C}(X,Y)}\otimes\bar{\rho}_{X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.24994pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.24994pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,Y)\otimes\mathcal{C}(X,X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 162.01881pt\raise 6.43889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.46112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu_{X,X,Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 198.26373pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 198.26373pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,Y).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proof.

One can check that

-\circledast X:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

is a well-defined enriched functor,
whose tensorial strength

a_{w,z,X}:=t^{-\circledast X}_{w,z}

at

,

in

\textstyle{w\otimes(z\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\otimes X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }a_{w,z,X}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\circledast X}

We also leave for the readers to check that

is a well-defined enriched functor.
The tensorial strength

,

becomes a morphism in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
which is the tensorial strength
associated to

.
The component of the adjunction unit at each

is the morphism

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
whose underlying morphism in
\mathcal{V}
is

\eta_{z}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.54515pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.54515pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.66103pt\raise 6.3618pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.38403pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textit{Cv}_{z,X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.54515pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.54515pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,z\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 100.8553pt\raise 6.61389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63612pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mdmathbb{I}_{X},\textit{cq}_{z,X})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.88533pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 145.88533pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(X,z\circledast X).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(3.2)

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
b
be a monoid in
\mathcal{V}

and let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete enriched category.
For each enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

we have an enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

between enriched functors from
\textit{Mod}_{b}
to
\mathcal{C}
,
whose component at
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

is the unique morphism

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{z}:z\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\to\mathcal{F}(z)

in
\mathcal{C}
which makes the following diagram commutative.

\textstyle{z\otimes b\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{\mathcal{F}(b)}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z\otimes b,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z\otimes b\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}({}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes m_{b})}
\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,\mathcal{F}(b)}}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}({}_{z})}
\textstyle{z\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{z}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)}

(3.5)

The component of
\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}
at
b\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

is the isomorphism

\imath_{\mathcal{F}(b)}:b\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\mathcal{C}

described in (
3.2
).
Moreover, the following are equivalent:

1.

The enriched functor
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

is a left enriched adjoint;

2.

The enriched functor
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

is cocontinuous.

3.

The enriched functor
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

preserves tensored objects,
and the underlying functor of
\mathcal{F}

preserves coequalizers.

4.

The enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

is an enriched natural isomorphism.

Proof.

For each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
,

\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(b\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z\otimes b\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)}

This shows that

is a well-defined enriched natural transformation.
From the defining relation
(
3.2
) of
\imath_{\mathcal{F}(b)}
,
we see that

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{b}=\imath_{\mathcal{F}(b)}:b\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{F}(b)
.

We are left to show that the statements 1-4 are equivalent.
The implications
1\Rightarrow 2\Rightarrow 3
and
4\Rightarrow 1
are immediate.
We prove
3\Rightarrow 4
as follows.
Since the enriched functor
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

preserves tensored objects,
the top and middle horizontal morphisms in the diagram (
3.5
)
are isomorphisms in
\mathcal{C}
.
Since the underlying functor of
\mathcal{F}

preserves coequalizers,
the right vertical morphisms in the diagram (
3.5
)
is also a coequalizer diagram in
\mathcal{C}
.
Thus we obtain that
\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{z}

is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}
for every

z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
∎

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
b
be a monoid in
\mathcal{V}

and let
\mathcal{C}
be a cocomplete enriched category.
We have a fully faithful left adjoint functor

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{-\circledast X:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}}

(3.6)

from the category of left
b
-module objects in
\mathcal{C}

to the category of
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
.
The essential image of the functor (

is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})

of cocontinuous
\mathcal{V}
-enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
,
and we have an adjoint equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

)

).

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\text{ }}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq EnEWThm leftadj})}
\textstyle{\text{ }\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq EnEWThm rightadj})}

(3.7)

For each
X\in\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}
,
the functor (

)
sends the enriched functor

to

whose left
b
-action morphism is

This shows that we have the adjunction
(
3.7
)
as we claimed,
whose adjunction unit is a natural isomorphism.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}

be an enriched functor.
The component
\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}

of the counit of the adjunction (
3.7
)
at
\mathcal{F}
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})

if and only if
\mathcal{F}

is a cocontinuous enriched functor,
by Proposition
3.2
.
We conclude that the left adjoint

\text{ }\!\!_{b}\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})

in (
3.7
)
is fully faithful, whose essential image
is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\mathcal{C})

of cocontinous enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\mathcal{C}
.
∎

For the rest of this section,
we examine the case when
\mathcal{C}

is the enriched category of right modules over another monoid in
\mathcal{V}
.
By considering this special case,
we obtain a result
more similar to the original Eilenberg-Watts theorem.
This is also a preparation
for the contents in the upcoming sections.

Let
b^{\prime}
be another monoid in
\mathcal{V}
.
A left
b
-module object in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
,
which is a right
b^{\prime}
-module
(x,{}^{\prime}_{x}:x\otimes b^{\prime}\to x)

equipped with a left
b
-action morphism

\rho_{x}:b\otimes x\to x
in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
We mostly omit
the right
b^{\prime}
-action
{}^{\prime}_{x}

as well as
the left
b
-action
\rho_{x}
,
and denote a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
(x,{}^{\prime}_{x},\rho_{x})
as
x
.
The category of
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules
is denoted as

\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

and for each
x\in\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

the associated enriched adjunction (
3.1
)
is denoted as follows.

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\circledast x}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}(x,-)}

(3.10)

The left enriched adjoint

-\circledast x:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

sends each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
to the coequalizer

\textstyle{z\otimes b\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\rho_{x}}
\textstyle{z\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,x}}
\textstyle{z\circledast x}

in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
and the tensorial strength at

w\in\mathcal{V}
,

z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

is the unique isomorphism

a_{w,z,x}:=t^{-\circledast x}_{w,z}
in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

\textstyle{w\otimes(z\otimes x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes\textit{cq}_{z,x}}

\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }a_{w,z,x}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\circledast x}

The following is a special case of Theorem
3.3
,
which exhibits the relation between
bimodules and (cocontinuous) enriched functors
between enriched categories of right modules.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be monoids
in a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}
.
We have a fully faithful left adjoint functor

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{x\text{ }\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\text{ }-\circledast x:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

from the category of
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules
to the category of enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
The essential image of this functor
is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

of cocontinuous enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
and we have an adjoint equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\text{ }\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Proof.

Substitute
\mathcal{C}=\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

in Theorem
3.3
.
∎

4
The six functors

In this section, we describe the six functors
associated to each morphism of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
A monoid
b
in
\mathcal{V}
is
commutative

if the product
m_{b}:b\otimes b\to b
satisfies the commutativity relation.
Throughout this section, we fix a commutative monoid
b
in
\mathcal{V}
.

Let
x
be an object in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
We have a morphism

\mathring{\gamma}_{x}:b\otimes x\to x

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

whose underlying morphism in
\mathcal{V}
is

\mathring{\gamma}_{x}:b\otimes x\xrightarrow[\cong]{s_{b,x}}x\otimes b\xrightarrow{{}_{x}}x
,
and
x
equipped with
\mathring{\gamma}_{x}

becomes a
(b,b)
-bimodule.
We denote the associated enriched adjunction
(
3.10
) as follows.

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\circledast x}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[x,-]}

(4.1)

The left enriched adjoint

-\circledast x:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

sends each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
to the coequalizer

\textstyle{z\otimes b\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\mathring{\gamma}_{x}}
\textstyle{z\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,x}}
\textstyle{z\circledast x}

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
and the tensorial strength at

w\in\mathcal{V}
,
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

is the unique isomorphism

a_{w,z,x}:=t^{-\circledast x}_{w,z}

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
which satisfies the following relation.

\textstyle{w\otimes(z\otimes x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes\textit{cq}_{z,x}}
\scriptstyle{a_{w,z,x}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{w\otimes z,x}}
\textstyle{w\otimes(z\circledast x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }a_{w,z,x}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(w\otimes z)\circledast x}

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
b
be a commutative monoid in a Bénabou cosmos
\mathcal{V}
.
We have the closed monoidal enriched category

(\textit{Mod}_{b},\circledast,b)

of right
b
-modules,
which is complete and cocomplete as an enriched category.
Let
x
,
y
,
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

1.

The coherence isomorphisms

a_{z,x,y}
,
\imath_{x}
,
\jmath_{x}

are unique isomorphisms in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

satisfying the following relations.

\textstyle{z\otimes(x\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,x\circledast y}}
\scriptstyle{a_{z,x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{(z\otimes x)\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,x}\circledast\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\textstyle{b\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{b,x}}
\scriptstyle{\mathring{\gamma}_{x}}

\textstyle{x\otimes b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{x,b}}
x

\textstyle{z\circledast(x\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }a_{z,x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{(z\circledast x)\circledast y}
\textstyle{b\circledast x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }\imath_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{x}
\textstyle{x\circledast b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }\jmath_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{x}

2.

The coherence isomorphism

s_{x,y}:x\circledast y\xrightarrow{\cong}y\circledast x

is the unique isomorphism in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
whose underlying isomorphism
in
\mathcal{V}
satisfies the relation below.

\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{s_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{y\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{y,x}}
\textstyle{x\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }s_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{y\circledast x}

In particular, the underlying closed symmetric monoidal category

\textit{Mod}_{b}
of right
b
-modules is also a Bénabou cosmos.

Proof.

The isomorphisms
a_{z,x,y}
,

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

are well-defined:
see §​
A.1
.
We leave for the readers to check that the isomorphisms

,
\jmath_{x}
in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
are well-defined,
and the inverses of their underlying isomorphisms in
\mathcal{V}
are

\displaystyle\imath_{x}^{-1}

Now we verify the pentagon, unital, symmetry and hexagon axioms.
The symmetry and unital axioms are explained in
§​
A.1
.
One can check the pentagon axiom as follows.

•

Let
w\in\mathcal{V}
.
The tensorial strengths associated to the enriched functors

-\otimes y:\mathcal{V}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

and

-\circledast y:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

satisfy the relations (
2.3
).
In particular, we have the pentagon axioms for the quadruples

w
,
z
,
x\in\mathcal{V}
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

and

w
,
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
x
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

•

Let
w\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
By pre-composing suitable epimorphisms,
the pentagon axiom for the quadruple

w
,
z
,
x
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

reduces to the pentagon axiom of

w\in\mathcal{V}
,
z
,
x
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
and further reduces to that of

w
,
z\in\mathcal{V}
,
x
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

Similarly, we obtain the hexagon axiom in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

as it reduces to the hexagon relation in
\mathcal{V}

after pre-composing suitable epimorphisms.
We omit the details.
We conclude that
(\textit{Mod}_{b},\circledast,b)

is a symmetric monoidal category.

For each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
we also have an enriched functor

z\circledast-:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

whose tensorial strength at

,

is
the unique isomorphism in
\textit{Mod}_{b}

satisfying the following relation.

\textstyle{w\otimes z\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(4.2)

The collection of enriched functors

z\circledast-
,
-\circledast x

give rise to an enriched functor

-\circledast-:\textit{Mod}_{b}\times\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
and the collection of enriched adjunctions
in (
4.1
)
uniquely determines the enriched functor

[-,-]:\textit{Mod}_{b}^{\op}\times\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
We leave for the readers to check that the coherence isomorphisms

a
,
\imath
,
\jmath
,

of
\textit{Mod}_{b}

are enriched natural in every variable.
We conclude that

(\textit{Mod}_{b},\circledast,b)
is a closed monoidal enriched category.
∎

A morphism
b\to b^{\prime}
of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}

is a morphism in
\mathcal{V}
which is compatible with unit, product morphisms of
b
,
b^{\prime}
.
We denote

\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})

as the category of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
For each morphism
f:b\to b^{\prime}
in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
,
we can see
b^{\prime}
as a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule and also as a
(b^{\prime},b)
-bimodule
where
b^{\prime}
-actions are the product
m_{b^{\prime}}

and
b
-actions are induced by
f
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
f:b\to b^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.
We have a pair of enriched adjunctions

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}=\text{ }\!-\text{ }\!\circledast b^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}=\text{ }\!-\text{ }\!\circledast^{\prime}b^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{!}=\text{ }\!\textit{Mod}_{b}(b^{\prime},-)}

and an enriched natural isomorphism

f_{!}\cong f_{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

1.

f^{*}=-\circledast b^{\prime}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is the left enriched adjoint
of the enriched adjunction
(
3.10
)
associated to the
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
b^{\prime}
.

2.

f_{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

sends each

to

f_{*}(y)=y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
whose right
b
-action is

\textstyle{{}_{y}:y\otimes b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{y}\otimes f}
\textstyle{y\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{\prime}_{y}}
\textstyle{y}

and the tensorial strength of

is the identity.

3.

f_{!}\dashv f^{!}

is the enriched adjunction (
3.10
)
associated to the
(b^{\prime},b)
-bimodule
b^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let

.

\textstyle{b^{\prime}\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{b^{\prime},b^{\prime}}}

\textstyle{b^{\prime}\circledast b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }m_{b^{\prime}/b}}
\textstyle{b^{\prime}}

Conversely, for each commutative monoid

we have a morphism

in

\textstyle{u_{b^{\prime}}:c\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{b}}
\textstyle{b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{b^{\prime},}

\textstyle{b^{\prime}\circledast b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{b^{\prime}/b}}
\textstyle{b^{\prime}.}

Let us denote

under

over

.
One can check that we have an isomorphism of categories

(4.3)

Proposition 4.3
.

The enriched adjunction

Proof.

We first show that

together with morphisms

,

is a lax monoidal enriched functor.
Let

,

.
We leave for the readers to check that
the morphism

(f_{*})^{2}
in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
is well-defined.
The morphism

is enriched natural in variable

A.2
.
We leave for the readers to check that

is compatible with

,
\jmath
,

of

and

,
\jmath^{\prime}
,

of

is also enriched natural in variable

of

and

of
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
:
see §​
A.2
.

is a well-defined lax monoidal enriched functor.
