29.85762pt\raise 7.13667pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.10336pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{b,b}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.86111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.86111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b\circledast b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.91655pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\jmath_{b})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.06937pt\raise-6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.69437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 116.69437pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Lemma 5.1
.

We have the functor
of evaluating at
b\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigl(b\xrightarrow{f(\mathcal{F})}\mathcal{F}(b)\xleftarrow{f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})}b^{\prime}\bigr)}

(5.3)

which sends each lax monoidal enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

to the following object

(f(\mathcal{F}),f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
.

1.

We have a commutative monoid
\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\mathcal{V}
whose unit, product morphisms are

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{F}(b)}

\displaystyle:\!\!\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.16377pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{c\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.61397pt\raise 5.4125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.6014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u_{b^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 35.16377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.55482pt\raise 6.0375pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0236pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u_{\mathcal{F}(b)/b^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.26097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.26097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b),}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

\displaystyle m_{\mathcal{F}(b)}

\displaystyle:\!\!\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 26.59724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.59724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b)\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.82486pt\raise 7.19225pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.60336pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{F}(b),\mathcal{F}(b)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.59724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-2.04439pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.59724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b)\circledast^{\prime}\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.4293pt\raise 6.0375pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0236pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m_{\mathcal{F}(b)/b^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 162.31947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 162.31947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

2.

We have a morphism

f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})=u_{\mathcal{F}(b)/b^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}^{0}:b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.

3.

We have a morphism

f(\mathcal{F}):b\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})

whose underlying morphism in
\mathcal{V}
is

\textstyle{f(\mathcal{F}):b\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\jmath_{b}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{b\otimes c\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\otimes u_{\mathcal{F}(b)}}
\textstyle{b\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b).}

(5.4)

Here,

\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}=\mathcal{F}(m_{b})\circ t^{\mathcal{F}}_{b,b}:b\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\to\mathcal{F}(b)

is the left
b
-action
defined in (
3.4
).

Proof.

We will show that the functor (
5.3
)
is well-defined on objects,
and leave for the readers to check the rest of the details.
From the isomorphism of categories

\textit{Comm}_{b^{\prime}}\cong\textit{Comm}(\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

described in (
4.3
),
the object

\mathcal{F}(b)
in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})

and the morphism

f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F}):b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
are well-defined.
Let us denote the morphism in (
5.4
) as

f:b\to\mathcal{F}(b)
.
Using the left
b
-action relations of
\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}
,
one can check that

f\circ u_{b}=u_{\mathcal{F}(b)}:c\to\mathcal{F}(b),\qquad f\circ m_{b}=\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}\circ(\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\otimes f):b\otimes b\to\mathcal{F}(b).

To conclude that
f:b\to\mathcal{F}(b)

is a morphism in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
,
it suffices to show that

m_{\mathcal{F}(b)}\circ(f\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b})=\rho_{\mathcal{F}(b)}:b\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\to\mathcal{F}(b).

This is shown in §​
A.3
.
Thus the functor (
5.3
) is well-defined on objects.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

be a lax monoidal enriched functor
and denote

(f,f^{\prime})=(f(\mathcal{F}),f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

as the object in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

described in Lemma
5.1
.
Then the enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

defined in Proposition
3.2

becomes a monoidal enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

between lax monoidal enriched functors
from
\textit{Mod}_{b}
to
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule

\mathcal{F}(b)

described in (
3.4
)
is the same as the
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule

\mathcal{F}(b)

whose actions are induced by
f
,
f^{\prime}
.
Thus we have

-\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)=f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}

as enriched functors from
\textit{Mod}_{b}
to
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
Moreover, we have the relation

\vbox{\hbox{
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.8822pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.5486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.8916pt\raise 1.69305pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.12001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}^{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 65.48898pt\raise-24.01375pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-33.8822pt\raise-14.75446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*})^{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-19.30554pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{b\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.26732pt\raise-25.1006pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.50832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{b}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.30554pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.30554pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}\qquad\quad\begin{aligned} &\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{b}\circ(f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*})^{0}\\
&=\imath_{\mathcal{F}(b)}\circ(\imath_{\mathcal{F}(b)})^{-1}\circ u_{\mathcal{F}(b)/b^{\prime}}\\
&=u_{\mathcal{F}(b)/b^{\prime}}=\mathcal{F}^{0}:b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b).\end{aligned}

We are left to show the following relation for all

x
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.

\textstyle{(x\circledast\mathcal{F}(b))\circledast^{\prime}(y\circledast\mathcal{F}(b))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*})^{2}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{x}\circledast^{\prime}\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(x)\circledast^{\prime}\mathcal{F}(y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}^{2}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{(x\circledast y)\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{x\circledast y}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(x\circledast y)}

This is shown in §​
A.4
.
∎

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
We have a fully faithful left adjoint functor

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{\bigl(b\xrightarrow{f}\tilde{b}\xleftarrow{f^{\prime}}b^{\prime}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

from the category of commutative monoids
in
\mathcal{V}
over
b\otimes b^{\prime}

to the category of lax monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
The essential image of this functor
is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

of cocontinuous lax monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
and we have an adjoint equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\text{ }\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

Proof.

We claim that the functor

\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

in (
4.6
)
is left adjoint to the functor

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)\to\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

in (
5.3
).

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq SixFunctors MainThm leftadj})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq MainThm rightadj})}

(5.5)

•

Let
(f,f^{\prime}):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to\tilde{b}

be an object in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
.
The object

b\circledast\tilde{b}
in
\mathcal{V}

has a unique structure of a commutative monoid in
\mathcal{V}

such that the isomorphism

\imath_{\tilde{b}}:b\circledast\tilde{b}\xrightarrow{\cong}\tilde{b}

in
\mathcal{V}

becomes an isomorphism
in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.
One can check that the functor

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)\to\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

in (
5.3
) sends
the lax monoidal enriched functor

f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

to the object

\bigl(\imath^{-1}_{\tilde{b}}\circ f,\imath^{-1}_{\tilde{b}}\circ f^{\prime}\bigr):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to b\circledast\tilde{b}

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
.
The component of the unit of the adjunction (
5.5
)
at each object
(f,f^{\prime}):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to\tilde{b}
in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

is the isomorphism

\imath^{-1}_{\tilde{b}}:\tilde{b}\xrightarrow{\cong}b\circledast\tilde{b}

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
.

•

The component of the counit of the adjunction (
5.5
)
at each lax monoidal enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is the monoidal enriched natural transformation

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})_{*}f(\mathcal{F})^{*}\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

described in Proposition
5.2
.
By Proposition
3.2
,

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}
is a monoidal enriched natural isomorphism
if and only if

\mathcal{F}
is cocontinuous (as an enriched functor).

In the proof of Theorem
3.3
,
which is presented in § ​
3
,
we already showed that
\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}
is natural in variable
\mathcal{F}
,
and that the unit, counit satisfy the triangle relations
(
3.9
).
Thus we obtain the adjunction
(
5.5
) as we claimed.
Since the adjunction unit is a natural isomorphism,
we further conclude that the left adjoint

\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

is fully faithful whose essential image is the coreflective full subcategory

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

of cocontinous lax monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}
.
We have an equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\Hom_{\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})}(b,b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\text{-}\textit{Funct}^{\textit{strong}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{f:b\to b^{\prime}\text{ }\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\text{ }f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

from the discrete category of morphisms

b\to b^{\prime}
of commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}

to the category of cocontinuous strong monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Let us denote

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{strong}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

as the category of cocontinuous strong monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
which is a full subcategory of

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)
.
Moreover, regard the set

\Hom_{\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})}(b,b^{\prime})

as a discrete category.
We claim that the functor

\textstyle{\Hom_{\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})}(b,b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{strong}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{f:b\to b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

(5.6)

is an equivalence of categories.
Let
f_{1}
,
f_{2}:b\to b^{\prime}
be morphisms in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.
By Theorem
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{strong}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}(f_{1}^{*},f_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}(f_{1}^{*},f_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}}\bigl(b\otimes b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(f_{1},I_{b^{\prime}})}b^{\prime},\text{ }b\otimes b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(f_{2},I_{b^{\prime}})}b^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\{*\}&\text{ if }f_{1}=f_{2},\\
\varnothing&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

This shows that the functor (
5.6
) is fully faithful.

We are left to show that the functor (
5.6
) is essentially surjective.
Let
\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

be a cocontinuous strong monoidal enriched functor.
By Lemma
5.1
,
we have an object

(f,f^{\prime})=(f(\mathcal{F}),f^{\prime}(\mathcal{F})):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
,
where
f^{\prime}=\mathcal{F}^{0}:b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{F}(b)

is an isomorphism in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.
Moreover,

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}:f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}\cong\mathcal{F}

is a monoidal enriched natural isomorphism
by Proposition
3.2
and Proposition
5.2
.
Let us denote

\tilde{f}:=f^{\prime-1}\circ f:b\to b^{\prime}.

We have an object

(\tilde{f},\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}}):b\otimes b^{\prime}\to b^{\prime}

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

and an isomorphism

f^{\prime}:b^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}
.
The image of this isomorphism
under the functor (
4.6
)
is a monoidal enriched natural isomorphism

\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}*}\tilde{f}^{*}\cong f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}
.
Thus we have

\tilde{f}^{*}=\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}*}\tilde{f}^{*}\cong f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*}\cong\mathcal{F}
.
This shows that the functor (
5.6
) is essentially surjective.
∎

6
An approach via Day convolution

Throughout this section, we fix commutative monoids
b
,
b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{V}

and explain an alternative way of obtaining
the equivalence of categories

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}

(6.1)

described in
Theorem
5.3
.
Consider the following equivalence of categories.

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}
\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{-\circledast x:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}}

(6.2)

We have the symmetric monoidal category
of right
b\otimes b^{\prime}
-modules

(\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}},\widetilde{\circledast},b\otimes b^{\prime}).

Meanwhile, the category

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

also has a symmetric monoidal category structure,
whose monoidal product is given by Day convolution.
The goal of this section is to show that
the equivalence of categories (
6.2
)
is compatible with symmetric monoidal category structures on both sides.
As a consequence, we obtain an equivalence

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}(\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}

(6.3)

by considering the categories of commutative monoids on both sides
of (
6.2
).
Together with the isomorphism of categories

\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\cong\textit{Comm}(\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}})

explained in (
4.3
),
we deduce the desired equivalence of categories (
6.1
).

Recall that the Day convolution of enriched functors

\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is the left Kan extension

\mathcal{H}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

of the enriched functor

\circledast^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}):\textit{Mod}_{b}\times\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

along
\circledast:\textit{Mod}_{b}\times\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
We denote the universal enriched natural transformation as

\theta_{-,-}:\mathcal{F}(-)\circledast^{\prime}\mathcal{G}(-)\Rightarrow\mathcal{H}(-\circledast-)
.
Day convolutions and their properties were first studied by B. Day in

Day 1970a
,
Day 1970
.

\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\times\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circledast}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}\times\mathcal{G}}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{H}}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\times\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circledast^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\theta}

Let
x
,
y
be
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules.
We are going to describe the Day convolution of enriched functors

-\circledast x
,
-\circledast y:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
Consider
x
,
y
as right
b\otimes b^{\prime}
-modules
and denote their right
b\otimes b^{\prime}
-actions as

\widetilde{\gamma}_{x}:x\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}\to x
,

\widetilde{\gamma}_{y}:y\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}\to y
.
The right
b\otimes b^{\prime}
-module
x\widetilde{\circledast}y

is given by the following coequalizer.

\textstyle{x\otimes b\otimes b^{\prime}\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\gamma}_{x}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{x}\otimes\mathring{\widetilde{\gamma}}_{y}}
\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}

We can also see
x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y
as a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
whose actions
\rho_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}
,

{}^{\prime}_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}
of
b
,
b^{\prime}
are unique morphisms in
\mathcal{V}

satisfying the following relations.

\textstyle{b\otimes x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\otimes\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{x}\otimes I_{y}}
\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\otimes y\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{x}\otimes{}^{\prime}_{y}}
\textstyle{b\otimes(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }\rho_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}}
\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}
\textstyle{(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }{}^{\prime}_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}}

Let us denote the coprojections
of the coproduct
b\otimes b^{\prime}
of
b
,
b^{\prime}
in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})

as
p:b\to b\otimes b
and
p^{\prime}:b^{\prime}\to b\otimes b
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
Proposition
4.3

we have lax monoidal enriched functors

p_{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\to\textit{Mod}_{b}

and

p^{\prime}_{*}:\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

whose coherence morphisms
(p_{*})^{2}
,
(p^{\prime}_{*})^{2}

satisfy the following relations in
\mathcal{V}
.

\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p_{*})^{2}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p^{\prime}_{*})^{2}_{x,y}}

\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}

\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}

(6.4)

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
x
,
y
be
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules.
The Day convolution of enriched functors

-\circledast x
,
-\circledast y:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is the enriched functor

-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y):\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
The component of the universal enriched natural transformation

\theta_{-,-}:(-\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(-\circledast y)\Rightarrow(-\circledast-)\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)

at
w
,
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

is the unique morphism

\theta_{w,z}
in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

whose underlying morphism in
\mathcal{V}

satisfies the following relation.

\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\otimes(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{w\circledast x,z\circledast y}}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{w\circledast x,z\circledast y}}
\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{w,z}}
\scriptstyle{\exists!}
\textstyle{w\circledast x\circledast z\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\circledast s_{x,z}\circledast\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{w\circledast z\circledast x\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w\circledast x}\circledast(p_{*})^{2}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{w\circledast z\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)}

(6.5)

Proof.

We leave for the readers to check that
the enriched natural transformation

\theta
is well-defined.
We verify the universal property of
\theta
as follows.
Suppose we are given an enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

and an enriched natural transformation

\alpha_{-,-}:(-\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(-\circledast y)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\circledast-)
.
We need to show that there exists a unique
enriched natural transformation

\xi:-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

which satisfies the relation

\alpha_{-,-}=\xi_{-\text{ }\!\circledast\text{ }\!-}\circ\theta_{-,-}
.

Consider the morphism

\phi:x\circledast^{\prime}y\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in

defined as

\textstyle{\phi:x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(b\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\jmath_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b).}

We have a well-defined morphism

of
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules
whose underlying morphism in
\mathcal{V}

\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

and the composition

\xi:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.34956pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.34956pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.50832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.78014pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 98.78014pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is the unique enriched natural transformation
that we are looking for:
see §​
A.5
for the details.
We conclude that the enriched functor

-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y):\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

together with the enriched natural transformation
\theta

is the Day convolution of enriched functors

-\circledast x
,
-\circledast y:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
∎

As we have
Proposition
6.1

and the adjoint equivalence of categories in
Corollary
3.4
,
we explicitly see that the Day convolution of
two cocontinuous enriched functors
from
\textit{Mod}_{b}
to
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

exists, and is also cocontinuous.
Therefore the category

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

has a symmetric monoidal category structure,
whose unit object is

\textit{Mod}_{b}(b,-)\otimes b^{\prime}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

and whose monoidal product is given by Day convolution.
The category of commutative monoids
in this symmetric monoidal category
is equivalent to the category

\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

of cocontinuous lax monoidal enriched functors

\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
The analogous results for enriched functors
from a small monoidal enriched category
to
\mathcal{V}

are explained in

(
Day 1970a
, §​ 3)
,

(
Loregian 2021
, §​ 6.2)
.

We claim that the equivalence of categories

\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

in (
6.2
)
is compatible with symmetric monoidal category structures on both sides.
The unit object in
\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}

corresponds to the
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule

b\otimes b^{\prime}

whose actions of
b
,
b^{\prime}
are

m_{b}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}}
and
\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\otimes m_{b^{\prime}}
.
For each
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
we have an isomorphism

e_{z,b,b^{\prime}}:z\circledast(b\circledast b^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\cong}z\otimes b^{\prime}

in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

which is the unique morphism satisfying the relation below.

\textstyle{z\otimes(b\otimes b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{z,b\otimes b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{a^{\prime}_{z,b,b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(z\otimes b)\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{z\circledast(b\otimes b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }e_{z,b,b^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{z\otimes b^{\prime}}

We also have an isomorphism

\bar{\gamma}_{z}:z\xrightarrow{\cong}\textit{Mod}_{b}(b,z)

in
\mathcal{V}
which corresponds to the morphism

{}_{z}:z\otimes b\to z
in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
One can check that we have an enriched natural isomorphism

\textstyle{-\circledast(b\otimes b^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\textit{Mod}_{b}(b,-)\otimes b^{\prime}}

(6.6)

between enriched functors from
\textit{Mod}_{b}
to
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
whose component at
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
is

(\bar{\gamma}_{z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}})\circ e_{z,b,b^{\prime}}
.
As we have Proposition
6.1

and the enriched natural isomorphism
(
6.6
),
we conclude that the equivalence of categories

\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)

is compatible with symmetric monoidal category structures on both sides.
Thus we have

\textstyle{\textit{Comm}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq SixFunctors Commb=CommMb})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\textit{Comm}(\textit{Mod}_{b\otimes b^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{eq DayConvolution laxadjunction})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\textit{-Funct}^{\textit{lax}}_{\textit{cocon}}(\textit{Mod}_{b},\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\!)}

which is another way of obtaining
the equivalence of categories
(
6.1
)
as explained in the beginning of this section.

We leave for the readers to check the last equality

\textstyle{{}^{\prime}_{x\circledast b^{\prime}}\circ(\eta^{*}_{x}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{b^{\prime}})=\textit{cq}_{x,b^{\prime}}:x\otimes b^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{x\circledast b^{\prime}}

using the relation (
4.4
).
This shows that
the morphisms

(\mathring{f}^{*})^{2}
,

(f^{*})^{2}

in
\mathcal{V}

are inverse to each other.

Lastly, we show that
f^{*}\dashv f_{*}

satisfies the projection formula:
see (
2.1
).
Let
x\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
y\in\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

and recall the
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
b^{\prime}
.
We can see
y
as a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule
whose left
b
-action is the opposite action
\mathring{\gamma}_{y}

of
f_{*}(y)\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
We can also see
b^{\prime}\circledast^{\prime}y

as a
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodule in an analogous way,
and
\imath^{\prime}_{y}:b^{\prime}\circledast^{\prime}y\xrightarrow{\cong}y

becomes an isomorphism in
\text{ }\!\!_{b}\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
We leave for the readers to check that the following isomorphism in
\mathcal{V}

exists.

\textstyle{x\otimes(b^{\prime}\circledast^{\prime}y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }a_{x,b^{\prime},y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(x\circledast b^{\prime})\circledast^{\prime}y}

\textstyle{(x\circledast b^{\prime})\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(x\circledast b^{\prime})\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(x\circledast b^{\prime})\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(x\circledast b^{\prime})\circledast^{\prime}y}

In particular,
f^{*}\dashv f_{*}
satisfies the projection formula.

A.4

5.2

We omit obvious brackets when describing objects,
and keep the information of the omitted brackets when describing morphisms.
We maintain the position of brackets in each of the vertical compositions.
We used the defining relations (
3.5
) of

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{x}
,

\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{y}

as well as the relation (
5.1
).
We leave for the readers to check the relation

\textstyle{({}_{x}\circledast\mdmathbb{I}_{y})\circ a_{x,b,y}=\textit{cq}_{x,y}\circ(\mdmathbb{I}_{x}\otimes\imath_{y}):x\otimes(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{x\circledast y.}

We continue our calculation as follows.

We used the defining relation
(
4.2
)
of the tensorial strength

t^{\mathcal{F}(b)\circledast^{\prime}-}

associated to
the enriched functor

\mathcal{F}(b)\circledast^{\prime}-:\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
,
as well as the relation (
5.2
).
We leave for the readers to check the relation

\textstyle{\imath_{y\otimes b}\circ t^{b\circledast-}_{y,b}=\mdmathbb{I}_{y}\otimes\jmath_{b}:y\otimes(b\circledast b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{y\otimes b.}

Upto this point, we treated

\mathcal{F}(b)
as the underlying object in
\mathcal{V}

of the right
b^{\prime}
-module.
From now on, we will treat

\mathcal{F}(b)
as the underlying object of the right
b
-module,
whose right
b
-action is induced from the morphism

f:b\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\textit{Comm}(\mathcal{V})
.
We further calculate as follows.

We used the description (
4.5
)
of the morphism
(f^{\prime}_{*}f^{*})^{2}_{x,y}
.
After right-cancelling the epimorphisms

\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x\otimes\mathcal{F}(b),y\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)}
,

\textit{cq}_{x,\mathcal{F}(b)}\circledast^{\prime}\textit{cq}_{y,\mathcal{F}(b)}

in our calculation,
we obtain the relation (
A.2
).

A.5
Proposition
6.1

Let
b
,
b^{\prime}
be commutative monoids in
\mathcal{V}

and let
x
,
y
be
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules.
We verify the universal property of the enriched natural transformation

\theta
described in Proposition
6.1
.
Suppose we are given an enriched functor

\mathcal{F}:\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

and an enriched natural transformation

\alpha_{-,-}:(-\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(-\circledast y)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}(-\circledast-)
.
Let
v\in\mathcal{V}
,
w
,
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
We have the following relations
since
\alpha_{w,z}

is enriched natural in variables
w
,
z
.

\textstyle{v\otimes(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{v}\otimes\alpha_{w,z}}
\textstyle{(v\otimes w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{v\otimes w,z}}
\textstyle{v\otimes\mathcal{F}(w\circledast z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{v,w\circledast z}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(v\otimes w\circledast z)}

(A.3)

\textstyle{v\otimes(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}-}_{v,z\circledast y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{v}\otimes\alpha_{w,z}}

\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(v\otimes z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{w,v\otimes z}}
\textstyle{v\otimes\mathcal{F}(w\circledast z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{v,w\circledast z}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(v\otimes w\circledast z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(t^{w\circledast-}_{v,z})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(w\circledast(v\otimes z))}

(A.4)

Recall that we defined the following morphism in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.

\textstyle{\phi:x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\imath^{-1}_{x}\circledast^{\prime}\imath^{-1}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(b\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{b,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b\circledast b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\jmath_{b})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b)}

Using the relations
(
A.3
)
and
(
A.4
),
one can check that the underlying morphism

\phi:x\circledast^{\prime}y\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\mathcal{V}

satisfies the relations below.

\textstyle{w\otimes x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{w,x}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{w\circledast x,y}}
\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w\circledast x}\circledast^{\prime}\imath^{-1}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{(w\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{w,b}}
\textstyle{w\otimes x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes\phi}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(w\circledast b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\jmath_{w})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{w\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{w,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(w\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}({}_{w})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(w)}

(A.5)

\textstyle{x\otimes z\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{x,z}\otimes\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{x}\otimes\textit{cq}_{z,y}}
\textstyle{x\otimes(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,z\circledast y}}
\textstyle{x\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\imath^{-1}_{x}\circledast^{\prime}\mdmathbb{I}_{z\circledast y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{z\otimes x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,y}}

\textstyle{(b\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(z\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{b,z}}
\textstyle{z\otimes x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\otimes\phi}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b\circledast z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\imath_{z})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{z\otimes\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\mathcal{F}}_{z,b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z\otimes b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}({}_{z})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)}

(A.6)

We claim that there exists a (unique) morphism

\psi:x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y\to\mathcal{F}(b)

of
(b,b^{\prime})
-bimodules, whose underlying morphism
in
\mathcal{V}
satisfies the following relation.

\textstyle{x\otimes y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\circledast^{\prime}y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists!\text{ }\psi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(b)}

(A.7)

The following continued diagrams show that
the morphism

\psi:x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y\to\mathcal{F}(b)

in
\mathcal{V}
is well-defined.

.

.

-actions.

We leave for the readers to check the relation

\textstyle{({}_{w}\circledast\mdmathbb{I}_{x})\circ a^{\prime}_{w,b,x}\circ(\mdmathbb{I}_{w}\otimes\imath^{-1}_{x})=\textit{cq}_{w,x}:w\otimes x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{w\circledast x}

in
\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}
.
After right-cancelling the epimorphisms

\textit{cq}_{w,x}\otimes\textit{cq}_{z,y}

and

\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{w\circledast x,z\circledast y}

in our calculation,
we obtain that

\alpha_{w,z}=\xi_{w\circledast z}\circ\theta_{w,z}
.
This shows the existence of
the enriched natural transformation

\xi:-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

We are left to show the uniqueness of
\xi
.
The component
\theta_{b,b}
of
\theta
at
b
,
b

satisfies the relation

\textstyle{(b\circledast x)\circledast^{\prime}(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\imath_{x}\circledast^{\prime}\imath_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{b,b}}

\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\circledast\imath_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{b\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\imath_{x\widetilde{\circledast}y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\scriptstyle{(p^{\prime}_{*})^{2}_{x,y}}
\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}

which we obtain by right-cancelling the epimorphism

\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{b\circledast x,b\circledast y}

in the diagram below.

\textstyle{(b\circledast x)\otimes(b\circledast y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\theta_{b,b}}
\textstyle{b\circledast x\circledast b\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{b}\circledast s_{x,y}\circledast\mdmathbb{I}_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\scriptstyle{\imath_{x}\circledast^{\prime}\imath_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{b\circledast b\circledast x\circledast y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\textit{cq}}_{x,y}}
\scriptstyle{\textit{cq}^{\prime}_{x,y}}

\textstyle{x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y}

The morphism

(p^{\prime}_{*})_{x,y}

is an epimorphism,
which follows from the defining relation (
6.4
).
Thus
\theta_{b,b}
is also an epimorphism.
Suppose we are given another enriched natural transformation

\zeta:-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\Rightarrow\mathcal{F}

which satisfies the relation

\alpha_{w,z}=\zeta_{w\circledast z}\circ\theta_{w,z}

for all
w
,
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}
.
By right-cancelling the epimorphism
\theta_{b,b}
in the relation

\xi_{b\circledast b}\circ\theta_{b,b}=\alpha_{b,b}=\zeta_{b\circledast b}\circ\theta_{b,b}

we obtain that

\xi_{b\circledast b}=\zeta_{b\circledast b}
.
Using the isomorphism

\imath_{b}:b\circledast b\xrightarrow{\cong}b

in
\textit{Mod}_{b}
,
we further obtain that
\xi_{b}=\zeta_{b}
.
Then we have
\xi_{z}=\zeta_{z}

for all
z\in\textit{Mod}_{b}

as we can see from the diagram below.

\textstyle{z\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{z}}
\textstyle{z\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigl(\lambda^{-\circledast(x\widetilde{\circledast}y)}_{z}\bigr)^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{z\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{z}}

\textstyle{z\circledast(b\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\circledast\xi_{b}\text{ }=\text{ }\text{ }\mdmathbb{I}_{z}\circledast\zeta_{b}}

\textstyle{z\circledast\mathcal{F}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{\mathcal{F}}_{z}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(z)}

We used the relation (
3.8
)
for
\xi
and
\zeta
.
Note that

\lambda^{-\circledast(x\widetilde{\circledast}y)}

is an enriched natural isomorphism,
since the enriched functor

-\circledast(x\text{ }\!\widetilde{\circledast}\text{ }\!y):\textit{Mod}_{b}\to\textit{Mod}_{b^{\prime}}

is cocontinuous: see Proposition
3.2
.
This shows that
\xi=\zeta
and that such
\xi
is unique.
This completes the proof of the universal property of
\theta
.
